## 2026-10-02 — a type-system, contracts and AI-docs review: every finding reproduced, then fixed

A review of the lattice, the checker, runtime contracts and the assistant-facing docs, with
every finding reproduced before it was filed (`docs/archive/review-2026-10-02.md`), then fixed in
five parallel workstreams split by file ownership. Each fix has a regression test at the
entry point a caller reaches, sabotage-verified red.

**Crashes.** `Ty::intersect` on two recursive types recursed without bound (a `deftype json`
checked against itself overflowed the stack): a meet pair that repeats now returns a sound
operand, and `json ∩ json` is answered exactly by an inclusion shortcut. A 20 000-link
`deftype` alias chain aborted the checker; past 256 links it reads as `any`.

**The lattice.** Union is an upper bound again (a record is not absorbed by a map shape whose
values exclude it). A projection takes its member's own length, a variadic arrow still
requires its fixed parameters, a length contradicting a tuple arity empties the member,
widening never narrows a subtraction or an arrow's domain, a bare `pair` parameter is no
longer consistent with everything, `map ∩ map` is exact, and `to_source` declines where it
cannot be faithful. Types are canonical across a new law corpus (`types/tests.rs` checks
reflexivity, transitivity, bounds, commutativity, absorption and `mutual ⊆ ⇒ ==` over lengths,
intervals, literals, list shapes, records with `map_kv`, optional/rest arrows and μ types).
A record instance is ⊆ `map<K,V>` over its declared fields, its hidden `:__id__` excepted —
as `keys`, `count`, `seq` and now the runtime contract all read it.

**Inference.** Loaded recursive closures get a widened self-ascent instead of a ⊥ self-call;
`merge` with an open later argument, `cons` onto a vector, `seq` (kind by kind over a union),
`range` with a step, record `count`/`keys`/`vals` and `nth` with a default match the runtime.
A guard alias dies with its target's rebinding; int-closed arithmetic is precise only over
precise operands; field truthiness and `contains?` select among closed-record alternatives;
`&optional` parameters get domains; keyword callbacks are checked; a lambda passed to an
arrow parameter is typed under that arrow (no strict false positive on `(fn (x) (+ x 1))`
against `(int -> int)`), and an immediately-applied lambda checks its arguments and arity.
Stale rule keys (`distinct`, `sin`/`cos`/`tan`, bare `quot`/`rem`/`mod`, `vector-length`,
`print`/`println`/`format`) are renamed, and `rule_heads_are_bound` fails if one goes stale
again. A same-file `number` function is re-typed under int arguments and `%add`/`%sub`/`%mul`
close like `+`/`-`/`*`, which removed the prelude's own `(inc slash)` false positive. The
soundness oracle now checks intervals, lengths, set elements and μ types.

**Lints.** `try` bodies are checked; only `error-of`/`assert-error` keep the KI-67
suppression, recognised by their expansion's shape. It found real defects in six std modules
(`dns` short reads, `serve`'s attach with no session, `codemod`'s nil root, and three smaller).
A dead clause is reported once, under `:unreachable-clause`; non-exhaustive literal matches,
no-method calls and arity errors gained `:type-mismatch` as their opt-out (they had none).
The `nest check` cache fingerprints the dispatch registries' content, so an impl added in
another file re-checks the file that declares the ability; the ability registry is filtered
to the file's own world, so a multi-file verdict no longer depends on order. Sealed-match
exhaustiveness forgets rebound names; `impl` ops read list parameter forms; variadic
behaviour ops accept any provider arity; the recursion and guard-effect lints respect
scope. `brood --check --strict` exists.

**Contracts.** Contracted non-tail recursion died near 4k levels armed against 200k unarmed:
the JIT's stack margin equalled `vm_apply`'s native reserve, so once the JIT declined a level
the VM refused it too. The JIT margin is 1 MiB, asserted at compile time to stay at least
twice the VM's. A shim declared `-> any` tail-calls the original; a return-checked shim
cannot, which is KI-216 (deferred — it needs a collapsing pending-check frame). `type-matches?`
reads a record name and a sealed ability nominally, as the checker does. Contract frames are
machinery in traces and positions. A thrown `{:kind <k> :message …}` map of a non-built-in
kind renders as `error: <k>: <message>` instead of the whole map. `seq/zip-with` is
data-first: `(zip-with a b f)`.

**Docs.** The writing-brood and brood-debug skills, `brood-for-claude.md`, `language.md` and
`error-codes.md` were re-verified against the binary and corrected (`spawn` of a `fn` does
run; `spawn-monitor` instead of the spawn-then-monitor race; `(doc inc)`; `:requires [A]`;
`lambda` is retired; eight special forms; deep recursion is a catchable E0044). Thirteen
citations of ADR-058 (GC) for mailbox-delivered window input now cite ADR-059.

**HEAD's CI was red, and is fixed here.** The v0.35.0 run failed two jobs. *Clippy (all
features)*: rustc 1.98's `clone_on_copy` and `extend_with_drain` in `process/message.rs`, and
an E0509 in `gui/backend/input.rs`'s test (moving a vector out of `Message`, which became
`Drop` for the iterative free) — taken with `mem::take`. *Tree-walker differential*:
`robustness_limits_test` "match, fn, let and receive all refuse it up front" — `receive`'s
pin and tag scans walked a 100 000-level pattern without a bound BEFORE the lowering cap
refused it, which exhausts the tree-walker's stack budget while the VM's heap frames
survived to the refusal; `receive` now calls the shared `%match-refuse-too-deep` before any
scan. And `sse_test` "a refused connection…" picked a port, released it and connected,
racing any parallel test's server for it — it connects to port 1 now, which no test process
can bind.

**Not done here:** KI-216; a caught contract error still carries no `:file`/`:line`/`:trace`
(adding them would break "what you throw is what you catch" for one kind — a design call);
`(map xs :kw)`, `comp` and `apply` callbacks are unchecked; a destructured `[tag v]` does not
narrow a record union. `exec_chunk.rs` and `jit_runtime/support.rs` changed near the call
path, so `make ab` and `make ab-vm` are owed on a quiet machine.

## 2026-10-03 — bedit's strict ratchet, and KI-216 closed (ADR-396)

**CI on `1a150b23` was red in one job: `downstream smoke (bedit @ BEDIT_REF)`.** bedit's
`strict_ratchet_test` (zero `nest check --strict` findings) read four — five at bedit's
HEAD. All five sat inside `try` bodies, which the 2026-10-02 review started checking. One
was an imprecise declaration HERE: `enclosing-call` (`std/editor/highlight.blsp`) was
declared `-> (or nil (vector (or map number string)))` for a value that is always
`[callee argi]`, so `reflect/read-string` saw a callee that might be a number or a map; it
is `(tuple string int)` now. The rest were bedit's own nil handling — a `json/decode` that
may not answer a map, a `(:proc state)` re-read after the `and` that tested it, a buffer
index tested through the buffer it found, an auto-save path for a buffer with no file —
fixed in bedit. bedit's format, check, `--check-boot` and the ratchet plus 560 neighbouring
tests are green against this tree; the full suite is CI's.

**KI-216.** A return-checked contract shim calls its original in argument position, so a
chain of contracted tail calls kept one suspended shim per call — `(int -> keyword)`
ping/pong hit the 1M frame cap armed and ran flat unarmed. The deferred fix was "a VM frame
kind that records a pending check"; it turned out a suspended shim already IS that frame,
and what was missing was a way to recognise one. The templates now call the original
inside `(%contract-await ret …)`, a `Prim2` mark whose resume point identifies the frame
and whose first operand — directly below the callee's frame — is the type it will check.
Before calling, `(%contract-tail? ret)`, a `Prim1` whose VM arm exits to the driver,
answers whether the frame this activation returns into is such a mark with an `equal`
type; if so the shim tail-calls. The tree-walker and nested runs answer `false` and keep
checking; both ops keep shims off the JIT. Nothing on the ordinary call path changed.

Guard `return_checked_tail_calls_collapse_and_still_check`: 2M levels at tiers 1 and 2,
a violation at the bottom of a chain, and a narrower type at either end of a chain.
Sabotaged both ways — never collapsing reds the flat case at tier 1, always collapsing
reds `narrow-inner` (it returned 3 unchecked). `contract_test` 115/115 at tiers 0/1/2
armed; the contracts cli/nest integration binaries, the prim unit tests, clippy on CI's
flags, rustfmt and `nest format --check` clean.

## 2026-10-05 — the checker residue from the 2026-10-02 review, closed

**`:only` names.** `(:use m :only [typo])` warns at the clause, naming the module and the
missing name — the half of KI-211 the runtime could not take (a module may be mid-load or
image-backed at refer time). Silent when the module could not be loaded at all.

**Callbacks.** Four shapes were never checked against what they are handed:
- a keyword — `(map ps :x)` now types as `(:x p)` per element (`infer::callback_ret`);
- a NAMED function — only `fn` literals were ever seeded with the element type, so
  `(map xs inc)` over strings was silent. A named, keyword or composed callback is now held
  to the same seed by disjointness, except over a record collection, which may iterate
  itself through `Seqable` (`record_test`'s `stack` was the false positive that showed it);
- `(comp f g)` — a signature composed from its stages, plus a finding when a stage returns
  what the next cannot take;
- `(apply f … xs)` — `f`'s fixed parameters against the leading arguments and the spread
  collection's elements.
`domains::a_permissive_higher_order_stdlib_callback_stays_silent` had pinned the old silence
on `(map [1 2 3] string/length)`, a program that raises; it now pins the finding.

**Narrowing.** The return check (`gradual_of`) recorded neither the path alias nor the
symbol alias a `let` makes, so every `match` over a tagged-tuple union with a declared
result read each arm's binder as the union — a strict false positive. And a destructured
`(let ([tag v] r) (if (= tag :ok) …))` never narrowed `v`: the matcher binds `tag` through
a temp, so the guard reached the path only via an alias, and `v`, bound before the guard,
never saw the base narrow. `narrow` now applies path narrowing to every alias the chain
reaches, `narrow_path` re-narrows the base's other positional aliases, and the three `let`
binders share `bind_pattern`.

**Smaller.** A finding inside a clause `match` copies into two branches was reported twice;
identical (position, message) pairs are now one. `defrecord` refuses anything after the
field list but `:derives [...]` — `(defrecord pt (x int) (y int))` defined fields `x` and
`int` and dropped `(y int)` silently.

Verified: `nest check` and `--strict` exit 0 over std/tests/examples and over bedit;
`types::` 692/692 with the new `check/tests/callback_shapes.rs`; the checker-facing `.blsp`
files green.

**Later the same day — the sabotage pass, and positioned contract errors.** All eight
`callback_shapes` guards were broken in three batches (keyword arm, `apply`, `comp` stage,
de-duplication and the `Seqable` guard; then the named-callback seed, the return check's
path alias and the sibling re-narrowing; then the alias-chain narrowing alone): each batch
reddened exactly the tests it targets and no other.

A caught contract error now carries a position. It was an ordinary `throw` of the policy's
map, so a `catch` saw no `:file`/`:line` where every built-in error has them; adding them at
the catch sites by `:kind` would have changed what a program's OWN `:contract`-kind throw
comes back as. Instead the policy raises through `%contract-raise`, which codes the error
`E0080`; a user `throw` never carries a code, so `LispError::caught_value` — now the one rule
the three catch sites share — enriches exactly those. Both halves of that are tested in
`contract_test` and both sabotage-verified (skip the enrichment: the position test reds;
enrich every map: the user-throw test reds). Under the tree-walker the position is the raise
inside `std/contract.blsp` rather than the user's call — only the VM filters contract
machinery out of positions (`is_contract_arm`); the caught map had no position at all
before, on either engine.

## 2026-10-08 — a whole-project review: forty-odd defects reproduced, then fixed

Six reviewers read every layer at `0f5ff008` — heap/GC, VM + JIT, scheduler + distribution,
builtins + host bindings, reader/checker/LSP, the stdlib — each reproducing a finding (or
tracing a concrete path, marked plausible) before filing it. Seven workstreams then fixed them
in this working tree, split by file ownership, each fix with a regression test at the entry
point a caller reaches and each test sabotage-verified red. The per-finding record — finding,
fix, guard — is `docs/archive/review-2026-10-08.md`; this entry is what it taught.

**Security first.** A dependency named `..` made a re-fetch `%rm-rf` the project itself: the
guard asked only that SOME path component be `_deps`, and no entry point (manifest, fetched
manifest, `nest add`, registry JSON) validated a name. Both layers are fixed — names are one
plain component, and `%rm-rf` refuses `..` and anything not strictly inside `_deps`. The
distribution handshake interned a peer's claimed node name before checking its MAC, so junk
connections could exhaust the wire symbol budget for good; the name is now bytes until
`ct_eq` passes. The HTTP server trusted framing (no body cap, `Content-Length` overrun kept,
`Transfer-Encoding` ignored, workers dying without a response); markdown's scheme filter and
`sse/frame` were injectable; `registry-install` took `file://`; wasm handles were guessable
and outlived their process; PBKDF2 truncated its iteration count to one round.

**Silent wrong answers, which is the class that matters most here.** A self-tail loop called
through an alias after a rebind ran the old body at tiers 1 and 2 — the hot-reload guard
assumed the name meant this arm when the frame began; frames now start at a sentinel epoch so
the first back edge checks (the native tier refuses such an arm, `self-loop-name-rebound`).
The linear-map rewrite accepted any value at the accumulator slot, so a loop that reset to
`{}` crashed. A closure as a map key was lost at the first collection because its hash was
handle bits a move rewrites — closures now carry a move-stable `identity`. The tree-walker's
`let` let a closure see a later binding. A self-reported "ok" from `agent/update` preceded the
update that killed it.

**What the gates missed, and why.** Every one of these was reachable from ordinary Brood; the
suite stayed green because each guard tested the piece its author wrote rather than the
caller's entry point — KI-97's quiet-subscriber reaping was tested by calling `clear_if`
directly while the death path never called it, and the tree-walker `let` bug is invisible to
`brood --test` at `BROOD_TIER=0` (test bodies do not reach the tree-walker), so its guard is
the Rust differential. The checker's new reserved-name lint passed its own tests and put 229
warnings on the prelude's own definitions — caught only by running the project's zero-warning
gate, not the feature's tests, the KI-106 lesson again; `boot::PRELUDE_PATHS` (pinned to
`PRELUDE` by a test) now tells the checker which files define those names.

**Breaking.** `proc/register` binds nothing for a dead pid. It briefly also refused a name a
live process held; that was reverted before landing, because every one of the 22 callers
(six in std, sixteen in bedit — a restarted logger, a test standing in as `:editor` and
handing the name back) registers precisely to take the name over. `csv/decode` raises on
an unterminated quote or junk after a closing quote. `%rm-rf` refuses `_deps` itself. Wasm
handles are opaque tokens. `proc/system-monitor` of a dead pid raises.

**Not done here, by decision:** inbound socket backpressure (a design question — the
recommendation is an explicit re-arm mode, as an ADR) and preferring a restarting peer's new
link (it would also tear down live links). Both are argued in the review record.

**Perf gate needed elsewhere** (no benchmarks on this machine): the self-call fix touches
frame entry and the first back edge per activation, and every closure creation now draws an
identity from a thread-local block — `make ab` and `make ab-vm` before this ships.

## 2026-10-09 — the review's CI fallout, and the nightly ASan job's eight days of red (KI-217)

The review's first CI run was red in three jobs, each a gate the local runs had not reached:
the wasm32 playground (the new `MAX_START_COLUMN` was `1 << 32`, which does not compile for a
32-bit `usize` — now 2^30, bounded as an `i64` before the cast), and the whole-suite ratchets
in both suite jobs (`*http-max-body-bytes*` unrecorded in `docs/bare-names.md`; `http_test`'s
new `brood-http-` temp prefix never purged). Everything else in the 1831-test suite passed in
both the VM and the tree-walker jobs.

Looking at the nightly for the full-suite verdict found it red since 2026-10-01 in the ASan job
— and the job stops at its first crash, so nothing behind that test had run under ASan for
eight days. Reproduced locally with a nightly toolchain: four symptoms (a decoder overflow, a
checker overflow, a TLS "unknown-crash", a SEGV inside Cranelift) were one cause — red zones
sized for release frames, which ASan makes several times larger — and are fixed in one place,
`crate::stack::maybe_grow`, now the only door to `stacker` (clippy enforces it). A fifth was a
real bug in every build: a root thread's retirement interned from a TLS destructor after the
symbol cache was gone, panicked, and never delivered its monitors' DOWNs. KI-217 has the
detail; the ASan lib suite is 1020/1020 at one and four threads.

**Then: no known issue left open.** The manual Nightly on `1052ffc1` ran the whole suite
under ASan clean (1588 s) and failed only one catalog case — `(defn identity …)` at the
root, which the new reserved-name lint correctly reports (the runtime refuses it at every
tier); it passed the earlier CI job only because whether a test heap seals the prelude
depends on its environment. Renamed. Every red full-suite job since 2026-09-24 (105 jobs)
was then read: none was KI-193's wave, which is closed, and three were one-off flakes no
entry described — a lockfile torn read that rolled back a concurrent `nest add` (KI-218,
reproduced 1 in 360), the stranded-work report losing a single `try_lock` race (KI-219,
7 in 480; an earlier "fix" had only loosened the test) and a ^C landing between `sh`'s fork
and exec (KI-220, already fixed by resending). The review's two deferred trade-offs are
closed too: inbound flow control for sockets and child output (ADR-397 — a credit in the
mailbox envelope, returned on drop, no API change; it surfaced KI-221, a TLS server
dropping bytes past rustls's 64 KiB), and a restarted peer that reconnects at once (an
incarnation in the handshake, wire v9). A string reader's parse error now names `<string>`
rather than inheriting the caller's file — a lockfile's `1:1` used to read as
`package.blsp:1:1`. Perf: ADR-397 adds a pointer to every queued envelope — `make ab` /
`make ab-vm` before release, with the rest of this review's hot-path changes.

## 2026-10-09 — the cleanup: dead flags, one tier knob, Brood-only tooling, docs archived

A whole-project review (eight areas) and a cleanup of everything it found: latent bugs, dead
switches and code, duplicated logic, giant functions, misattached docs, tooling in a language
the project does not use, and documentation that had become a second devlog. Every bug fix
below carries a regression test that was sabotage-verified (fix reverted → red → restored).

**Bugs fixed.**
- *Kernel:* `reset_local_to` dropped the source positions of OLD-generation pairs (the age bit
  at bit 32 was discarded by the retain); a failed `%std-image-reinstall!` replay no longer
  counts as a good imaged boot (falls back to source); a string blob can no longer be built
  from raw bytes (`SharedBlob::from_text` vs `new` — the `from_utf8_unchecked` safety argument
  was false); the image directory's capacity is capped by the bytes present.
- *Checker:* a bare `(fn)` argument inside a `:pure` function panicked (`properties.rs`).
  And the checker could fail to terminate (pre-existing; exposed when the sharper derivation
  built a recursive parameter type for `mcp_test`): unrolling a `μX` type for a subtype or
  disjointness question rebuilt its unions CANONICALLY, which asked more subtype questions of
  the same type, each with a pair the cycle guard had not seen — exponential in the number of
  alternatives. Relations now unroll with unions written as they stand
  (`unroll_for_relation`), and a hard backstop (`MAX_REC_UNROLLS` per outermost question,
  answering the sound "no") guarantees termination even if that regresses.
- *Processes/net:* `tcp/connect` resolved and dialled (10 s per address) ON a scheduler
  worker — now a helper thread, the Brood API still synchronous, failures still `E0050`; a node
  listening on `:0` advertised port 0 (now the bound address; a wildcard host advertises the
  node's own host); each exit swept NAMES twice and scanned every socket/subprocess/wasm
  registry (now one sweep and an owner index, O(owned)); `PARKED_COUNT` was Relaxed in a
  two-sided handshake (a lost wake on weakly-ordered CPUs).
- *std:* `format`'s file walker followed symlinks (a cycle never terminated) — five
  hand-rolled walkers are now `file/walk-files`; `dns/lookup` leaked its socket on every
  failed lookup; `csv` dropped a final `""` row and every bare `\r` inside a field;
  `gen/call-timeout` delivered a second reply after success; `string/blank?` was O(n²).
- *Tooling:* `nest publish` shipped `tests/` and the lock file (the `./`-prefixed tar excludes
  never matched `git ls-files` paths); `publish --bump` published after a failed git commit;
  the version bump edited the first `:version` in the text, not the project's; `nest ws`
  always exited 0; `nest run FILE -h` printed nest's usage instead of passing `-h` to the
  program; `nest test` decided failure by matching message text (now a structured
  `:suite-failed`); MCP `run-tests` and `nest test` discovered different test sets.
- *LSP/builtins:* checker findings with no position were dropped by the LSP; ~90 error labels
  named functions that no longer exist (now a test pins every label to a callable name);
  `%untar-gz` refused nothing (now rejects absolute, `..`, symlink and hard-link members); a
  corrupt release bundle silently ran as plain `brood`.
- *Gates:* `scripts/check-examples.sh` passed "10 failed"; `perf-brood`/`perf-symbols`
  overwrote the timing binary (now `target/release-perf/`, `target/release-symbols/`).

**Follow-ups (2026-10-12).** `seq/vector-ref` / `seq/vector-length` are deleted in favour of
`nth` / `count` (ADR-398, in the rename ledger). The LSP's "did you mean" ranking is Brood —
`fuzzy/edit-distance` and `fuzzy/nearest` — and the Rust Levenshtein is gone. bedit's two
leftovers (`%append-two`, `lineedit-init`'s ignored second argument) are fixed on bedit's side,
so both are removed here: `lineedit-init` is `(prompt history &optional opts)`. **bedit must
be pushed and `BEDIT_REF` moved with this change** (`make smoke-bedit ARGS=--bump`).

**Behaviour changes to know.** A `throw` inside an MCP tool handler reports only
`{kind, message}` in `error.data` (Brood's `catch` binds the value, not its position).
`package/bump-version` takes `(root level)`; `project-collect-tests` is `collect-tests`.
`%random-token`, `%print`, `%coverage-reset`, `%scope-live-pids` and `%mcp-progress`
are gone, as is the per-process eval deadline (MCP's Brood dispatcher kills a handler process
instead).

**Structure.** `jit_lower_arm_inner` 2 077 → ~560 lines; `check_forms` is a list of named
passes in run order; `seq_aware_call_ty` is one `match`; `check_into_inner` 882 → ~170; the
ability/multimethod walkers are one scope-aware walker (which now also sees calls in vectors
and under computed heads); deopt reasons are a `DeoptReason` enum with unique codes (the trace
prints names); one integer-arithmetic emitter; the heap verifier ~440 → ~240; the test-only
single-space collector is deleted; `handle_cmd`/`establish` are split; ~20 bespoke env
toggles moved into `debug_flags.rs`. Not done, deliberately: the two message-copy walkers
(they decline differently on a hot path that cannot be measured here) and `doc-catalog`
generation (its categories are editorial).

**Flags removed** (each with its read, its old code path and its catalogue entry):
`BROOD_VM` and `BROOD_NO_JIT` (the aliases — **`BROOD_TIER=0|1|2` is now the only tier
knob**), `BROOD_XADMIT`, `BROOD_MKCLO`, `BROOD_FLOAT_VETO`, `BROOD_XCALL`,
`BROOD_NO_DEOPT_RESUME`, `BROOD_NO_INLINE`, `BROOD_NO_LEAF_INLINE`, `BROOD_NO_PARTIAL_LEAF`,
`BROOD_NO_FLOAT_GLOBAL`, `BROOD_NO_SHARED_ARMS`, `BROOD_SPAWN_RR`, `BROOD_NO_MSGTAG`,
`BROOD_NO_HANDOFF`, `BROOD_NO_STEAL_WAKE`, `BROOD_NO_IDLE_BACKOFF`, `BROOD_TEST_NO_SCOPE`,
`BROOD_NO_SHARE_FN_MSG` (folded into `BROOD_NO_SHARE_FN`); `BROOD_COMPILE_TRACE` is renamed
`BROOD_TRACE_LOWER`. CI's tree-walker job, `make test-both`, the fuzz runner and the docs use
`BROOD_TIER`. Kept on purpose: `BROOD_NO_RECV_MARK` and `BROOD_NO_SHARE_FN` (their failure
mode is a silently undelivered message, so the switch earns its place) and `BROOD_MONO`
(opt-in; making it default is a perf decision for a machine that can measure it).

**`nest mcp` moved to Brood** (`std/tool/mcp.blsp`: transport, dispatch, the tool
catalogue); `crates/nest/src/mcp.rs` only supplies the documents the binary embeds. The Rust
subcommands left are `stdimage` and `release`'s byte assembly.

**Python removed.** The thirteen fuzz generators (`scripts/fuzz/generators/*.blsp`), the
stress program fuzzer and reader fuzzer, the table oracle (now bash) and the bench-ratio
parser are Brood or shell; `scripts/ki38/` (a closed investigation's tooling),
`scripts/fuzz_unbox.py` and the unreferenced `scripts/fmt-probe.blsp` are deleted.

**Gates.** Every Makefile target that runs tests puts `ulimit -v 16000000` in front of the
run step only (`RUN_CAP`), never the build; `gui-debug` is gone. A nightly `perf-stats` job
builds the counter-armed runtime and runs the JIT and differential binaries plus
`tests/perf_test.blsp` on it, and requires `BROOD_PERF_STATS=1` to dump a non-zero `vm_apply`
(checked: passes on a perf-stats build, fails on an ordinary one).

**Docs.** `CLAUDE.md` went from 991 lines to ~425: the 110-row flag table is replaced by
`brood --debug-flags` (substantive table-only facts moved into the catalogue's descriptions),
dated incident retellings are cut to their rules, and the perf narrative moved to
`docs/benchmarking.md` §6. Closed known-issue bodies moved to
`docs/archive/known-issues-closed.md` (the index, filing rules and open entries stay in
`known-issues.md`); `docs/archive/devlog-archive.md` is deleted (git keeps it);
`docs/handoff.md` is one current-state page instead of 4.5k lines of dated sections.

## 2026-10-12 — the cleanup's follow-through: tcp/close, error positions, the last curated overlap

- **`tcp/close` on a listener is synchronous.** It only queued the close on the reactor, so
  for a moment after it returned the listener still accepted — a peer could connect to a port
  the program believed closed. The listener fd is now shared by the registry entry and the
  reactor (`ListenerSlot`); `close` empties and closes it on the calling thread, and the
  reactor treats an emptied slot as gone. No blocking round-trip, so the worker never waits.
  Guarded by 300 close-then-connect rounds in `tests/tcp_test.blsp` (red 3/3 with the queued
  close restored, on one core). Streams are unchanged on purpose: a closing stream keeps
  reading through its linger so `send` + `close` never truncates a response.
- **A raise inside a `nest mcp` tool reports where it came from again.** `catch` binds the
  thrown VALUE, so a thrown string had nowhere to carry its position once the dispatcher moved
  into Brood. New kernel primitive `(%try-described thunk handler)` calls
  `(handler caught description)`, the description being the full
  `{:kind :message :file :line :col :trace}` map for ANY thrown value; MCP's error mapping uses
  it, so `error.data` carries `file`/`line`/`col`/`trace` for user throws as it did in Rust.
  The compiler only inlines one-parameter handlers, so the VM's catch path is untouched.
- **No curated signature duplicates a std declaration.** The derivation now types a
  self-call's arguments under the function's own parameter demands (the induction step: the
  calling activation was itself entered inside that domain), which proves `keyed-loop`'s
  `(- i 1)` an int; `nest/main` declares the fact only it knows, `((list string) -> int)`.
  With those, `math/even?`, `math/odd?`, `string/starts-with?`, `string/ends-with?` and
  `index-of` leave the curated table and `no_curated_name_is_declared_in_std` admits no
  exceptions.
- **The dated-comment sweep is finished** across `std/` and every crate (~810 more lines):
  history became the rule and its reason; invariants and safety arguments stayed.
- Proven: the distribution suite 3× on four cores, eight concurrency-heavy files under
  `BROOD_GC_STRESS=1 BROOD_GC_VERIFY=1`, a fuzz-differential pass (180 programs, six
  generators, zero divergences), the network files 90× across tiers and boot paths, both
  checker gates at zero, and bedit's three gates.

## 2026-10-12 — the cleanup's last four: tcp/close silence, one copy walker, devirtualization on, a derived doc catalogue

- **`tcp/close` silences the socket at once.** A closing stream lingers up to 5 s to flush
  queued output, and during that time the old owner could still receive inbound data and a
  late `[:tcp-closed]`. Each socket now has one owner record; every delivery goes through it
  under its lock, and `close` clears it under the same lock before returning, so nothing new
  for the socket reaches any mailbox once `tcp/close` returns. Queued output still flushes
  (send-then-close never truncates); bytes read meanwhile are discarded and their flow
  credit returned. The rule covers TLS streams, listener accepts, failed dials and the
  reactor-death sweep; `close` itself sends nothing. Guards: `tests/tcp_test.blsp` (8 MiB
  linger, 6 rounds; nothing queued, 30 rounds) and `tests/tls_test.blsp` (mid-request close)
  — the linger and TLS guards sabotage-verified.
- **One message-copy walker.** `to_message_rec` (Value → `Message`) and
  `copy_cross_heap_rec` (the L1 parked-receiver copy) were the same traversal written twice.
  They are `copy_value<T: CopyTarget>`, monomorphised over `IntoMessage` and `IntoHeap`;
  every difference (budget charging, up-front size checks, refusal as an error vs a silent
  decline, failures, closures by handle, builtins in image mode, list positions) is an
  `#[inline]` trait method, and refusals build their error lazily, so each path compiles to
  its own walker with no runtime mode test. The L1 vector arm no longer copies the element
  array. New `decline_tests` pin each asymmetry; the depth limit had no test before.
- **Ability devirtualization is always on; `BROOD_MONO` is gone (ADR-182/398).** Turning
  it on found five ways the opt-in rewrite disagreed with the op's own dispatch (KI-223).
  The rewritten head is `%dispatch-proven`, which re-checks the op's and constructor's
  definitions on every call and falls back to the op itself; calls with user-code
  arguments, rebound or wrapped op names, rejected arities and contract-armed runs are not
  rewritten. `crates/cli/tests/devirt_differential.rs` compares tiers 1 and 2 with the
  tree-walker. The trace is `BROOD_DEVIRT_DBG`. Tier 2 stays deferred; the performance
  effect is unmeasured here (a rewritten call trades the op's frame and `%identity-of` for
  one primitive with two cached reads).
- **The doc catalogue derives categories.** `std/tool/doc-catalog.blsp` no longer lists
  ~600 names by hand (695 → ~205 lines). A qualified name takes its module's category, a
  bare prelude name the category of the `;; ---- section ----` header above its definition,
  a kernel primitive a small table; 3 exceptions remain. `tests/doc_catalog_test.blsp`
  replaces the hand-list gates: every public name gets a category, every section key
  matches exactly one header, an exception must disagree with its section. Five names moved
  (`any?`, `every?`, `includes?`, `vec` → collections; `contains?` → maps). The prelude's
  source copy falls back to the temp dir when the cache dir is read-only, since categories
  now depend on its definition sites.

## 2026-10-12 — preemption reaches every shape of running code; nine latency and stability defects closed (ADR-400..405, KI-230..238)

The brief: "latency and stability is more important than pure performance — it has to be as
stable as can be." The brood-benchmarks `latency` row (20k req/s open loop, every 20th request
holding its worker ~500 µs) read p99 83–95 µs but p99.9 446–1 539 µs and max 2.3–4.1 ms run to
run, against Elixir's 66 / 213 µs. `BROOD_STALL_MS=1` showed a quantum over 1 ms for nearly
every fat request. Each finding below was reproduced, fixed and guarded in that order.

- **`try` bodies ran nested (KI-230, ADR-400).** `Inst::TryCatch` evaluated the body through
  `exec_value`, which the driver cannot capture: a loop in a `try` was one quantum at every
  tier and a `receive` in one blocked its worker — every `http/serve` handler. `try` is now
  bytecode in the arm's chunk (`TryEnter … TryExit`), with the catch in the driver
  (`catch_in_driver`, out of line: inlined at the driver's ten raise sites it cost `ring` 8%
  of its IPC).
- **Native code was preemptible only at a back edge the driver itself entered (KI-231,
  ADR-401).** Under a native caller a callee's preempt re-ran it on a nested interpreter: after
  500 warm spawns a 2.5 ms loop ran as one 10 ms quantum. A preempt now unwinds the native
  activations into interpreter frames: each caller's error path writes an in-call journal to
  the top of `roots` (a side record — a first version used the frame's checkpoint journal and
  gave pure self-recursive arms that area, which cost `bintree` 10%), a per-thread unwind
  record names the activations, and the driver rebuilds them. An entry reduction poll covers
  native recursion. Activations that cannot unwind keep running native (they used to deopt
  into an interpreter that could not preempt either). Residual by design: the unboxed register
  worker (demoting it costs ~45× on `fib`).
- **Preempt only for someone.** Unwinding a native stack, or stopping and re-entering a fold,
  is costly; with no process queued anywhere it buys no latency. Both now ask
  `scheduler::work_waiting` (one relaxed load) and otherwise run on as a fresh quantum, the
  `:long-schedule` timer restarted. Single-process compute rows stopped paying for preempts
  nobody needed (`json` +5.9% → +1.5% instructions).
- **`binding` bodies ran nested too (KI-232, ADR-402)** — inlined as `BindEnter … BindExit`,
  restored by truncation on a catch or a driver exit.
- **Vector/range folds were one quantum (KI-233, ADR-403)** — owed preemption, and resumable
  `%vector-reduce` / `%range-reduce` that stop between elements. Their primitive loops run in
  spans bounded by the inner loop's own compare: `reduce` reads 23% fewer instructions than
  before.
- **A process re-queued behind a long quantum waited it out (KI-234)** — at a 200-reduction
  quantum the median request waited 2 144 µs. Bounding the quanta fixed it (15 µs). A peer
  wake on every such enqueue was tried first and reverted: `supervisor` +12%, no latency
  gain once quanta were bounded.
- **A stale inlined native survived a redefinition (KI-235)** — found because `undef_test`
  failed 10/10 on the binary from before this work: the compiler stored a lowering that
  finished after the arm was reset, and its dedupe map stamped it with the new epoch.
- **`spawn` promoted each closure into the shared region (KI-236, ADR-405)**: the region's
  collector held the `promote_lock` writer for 100–300 µs; spawns now copy captured values
  into the child and keep the code shared.
- **A set, ratio or decimal literal put its function on the tree-walker for good (KI-237)** —
  the crash reporter's quantum never ended because `take-over` folds into `#{}`.
- **A run-check verdict recorded without the stdlib image replayed its loads forever
  (KI-238)** — after a `std/` edit a trivial program booted in 148M instructions instead of
  81M until the cache entry aged out; it read as an 8% `nqueens` regression until traced.
- **`:long-schedule` (ADR-404)** in the system monitor, and `make latency-gate`
  (`stress/latency_probe.blsp`, the benchmark box's tail gate).
- The sampling profiler samples a native loop that cannot yield (it used to see it only
  because such loops fell back to the interpreter every quantum).
- **Base RSS +27% in the brood-benchmarks refresh was transparent huge pages**, not memory:
  +1% with `MIMALLOC_ALLOW_THP=0` (docs/benchmarking.md §1.5).

**Result** (A/B against v0.37.0, `scripts/ab-bench.sh --floor`, flagged rows re-run alone
best-of-11): `latency` 4 789 → 2 551 ms (−47%); `make latency-gate` worst p99 115 → 80 µs;
at a 200-reduction quantum p50 86–3 263 → 15 µs. `reduce` −10%, `loop` −6%; `fib`, `pfib`,
`nbody`, `sort`, `supervisor` level. **Open cost:** `nqueens` +4.7%, `ring` +5.0%,
`bintree` +3.8% — instructions +1.6%, ~+1% and +4.6%; the remainder is lower IPC, not yet
attributed. Gates: `make test-both` 1829/1829 on both engines, `make gcstress` clean, fuzz
13 generators × 100 (1 900 programs) 0 divergences / 0 crashes / stale=0, clippy
`--all-features` clean.

## 2026-10-13 — the v0.38.0 release run: an https response mangled into text

The release commit's tree-walker job went red twice on two network tests, and the cause was
older than the cleanup. `tls/request` sends its request from the reactor, without the
caller, so the response can arrive before the caller's `tcp/set-binary` lands; under load
(the whole suite on the tree-walker) the first chunk of a binary https body was decoded as
UTF-8, every byte over 0x7F becoming U+FFFD. `http/request`'s comment claimed the mode was
set "before the request goes out" — true for plain TCP only. `%tls-request` takes an
optional fifth argument, `binary`, the response's starting mode, and `tls/request`,
`http/request` and the TLS tests pass it instead of switching afterwards. Guard:
`tests/tls_test.blsp` "a binary-mode TLS response is byte-faithful bytes" no longer calls
`set-binary`; with the argument ignored it reads `#b"\0\xef\xbf\xbd\xef\xbf\xbdA"`, and
`http_test`'s https binary case goes red with it.

**The second failure was the suite's one recurring flake, and it was not a timeout.**
`dns_test`'s "a well-formed reply decodes its A record" had failed the in-language suite's
FIRST try in four of the six CI and nightly runs since the cleanup, every one green through
nextest's retry, which only prints `FLAKY 2/2`. Its fake resolver sent the reply on accept,
without waiting for the query; `tcp/connect` returns with reads already started, so the reply
could land before `dns/ask` switched its socket to binary, decode as text, and fail to parse.
A real resolver answers only after the query, which `ask` sends after the switch. The fake
now waits for the query. Proven with a 200 ms `sleep` put between `ask`'s connect and its
`set-binary`: the old fake fails at exactly CI's line, the new one passes. (My first fix, a
longer client timeout, treated a symptom and is reverted.)

**Flakes now fail CI.** `scripts/nextest-no-flakes.sh` runs nextest and fails the job if any
test passed only on a retry, naming it; CI's test and tree-walker steps and the nightly suite
use it. Retries stay — "failed once" and "always fails" are still told apart — but a flaky
pass is no longer green.

**KI-224: the nightly sanitizer job's two failures were real.** Plain `cargo test` runs the
lib tests as threads of one process, so it is the one place two runtimes share a process, and
three process-wide states that belong to a runtime showed it: the held image file
(`OPEN_IMAGES`, KI-119's fix — a second runtime's index re-pointed the first's directory at a
rebuilt file), the `defdyn` marks (`value::DYNAMICS`, which `%isolate` restores wholesale over
the other runtime's marks; `stdimage/build` then refused on `*fuzzy-workers*`), and the image
temp file named by pid alone. Both now live on `RuntimeCode` (`image_files`, `dynamics`), and
temp names add a counter. The dist listener test read the process's first listener instead of
its own. Guards: two-runtime tests for the image handle and for the marks, each red with the
process-wide state put back; the threaded repro 0 in 15 after, 2 in 5 before.

**Also:** the wasm32 build broke on `tls_request`'s new argument (its stub `net_wasm.rs` was
not updated) — CI's wasm job caught it; two wasm-only dead-code warnings are gone, and two
doc comments in `scheduler.rs` that had been run together are on their own functions again.

**KI-225 — an exit signal killing the root program hung `brood file.blsp`.** The next CI run
went red in the breakage suite: `chaos2_process_links.blsp`'s P15 spawned a child and linked
it separately, and a child that had already finished made the link a `:noproc` exit signal to
the untrapping root. Reproducing it showed something worse than a racy test: the root killed
by an exit signal never exits — in `sleep`, `receive … after` or a compute loop, in 0.35 too.
The program runs as one green process and the main thread waits for it to publish an outcome,
which it did only on finishing or on a form error. Every scheduler death now goes through
`Process::retire`, which publishes for the root (`:normal` → exit 0, otherwise exit 1 "killed
by an exit signal: <reason>"), with a drop-time backstop. A related bug: a hard kill never
stopped a JIT'd loop, because native preemption skipped the VM's kill check; preemption now
honours it. P15 uses `spawn-link`. Guard: `crates/cli/tests/root_exit_signal.rs`, 9 shapes ×
3 tiers, each fix sabotage-verified. Its scripts first ordered steps by sleeping, and CI's
tree-walker booting from source outslept one; they are ordered by handshakes now.

**Three bugs the downstream update round found** (bringing ~25 projects to 0.38):
- *A project's format rules never reached `nest format`.* `project/setup` ran
  `(def *format-headers-extra* …)` inside the `project` module, and unless `format` had already
  loaded and marked the name dynamic, that bound `project/*format-headers-extra*`, which
  nothing reads — Hatch's `deflive`/`on`/`tick` shapes were ignored in every project, and
  hatch-demo's `nest format --check` failed on 11 correctly formatted files. `format` owns a
  setter now (`format/use-headers`). The unit tests asserted on the helper that computes the
  table; `crates/nest/tests/format_project_headers.rs` asserts on the file `nest format` writes
  (red before).
- *`node/monitor` failed its own contract.* Both its `sig` and the primitive's declared a `ref`
  result; it returns the node keyword, so under `nest run`/`nest test` every call raised —
  `reconnect`, `editor/serve` and the observer all call it. Every test of it ran through
  `brood`, which arms no contracts; `contracts_default.rs` now calls it under `nest run` (red
  before).
- *A private prelude name was reported as shadowed.* `(:use hatch/web/live)` warned that hatch's
  public `*live-registries*` shadowed the prelude's — a `def-`, unreachable from user code. The
  import check skips private names (`crates/cli/tests/import_shadow_warning.rs`, red before; a
  public name still warns).
- *`(:use m :only [Ability])` was "defines no public".* The checker's unknown-`:only` lint
  counted globals, and an ability is not one — `defability` registers its bare name in
  `*ability-owner*`. mail-local and mail-mailjet, which import `Adapter` so `(impl Adapter …)`
  reads bare, failed their zero-warning gate. The lint now also asks `*ability-owner*` whether
  the module declared that name (`modules_and_imports::an_only_import_of_the_modules_ability_is_not_named`,
  red before; another module's ability is still named).

## 2026-10-13 — a runtime-stability pass: per-runtime state, timed tests, a soak, and running out of descriptors

With nothing open in `known-issues.md`, the pass went after the classes behind recent reds
instead of single bugs. Four lines of work, three new known issues found and fixed.

**Per-runtime state (KI-226).** KI-224 moved three process-wide statics onto `RuntimeCode`;
an audit of the rest found two more holding one runtime's facts. The module resolver's
"failed to find" memo depends on `*load-path*`, so a miss in one runtime made another — whose
load path had the module — report it unbound; and the image kind index let one runtime's
image tell another that a macro was a function, so the macro call compiled as a call. Both
are `RuntimeCode` fields now, and the stdlib image's kinds ride the `SharedBundle` into each
runtime (the install runs once, in the shared boot; without the seed every runtime lost lazy
loading — `curated_names_load_nothing` caught that). `crates/lisp/tests/process_wide_statics.rs`
makes the choice explicit from now on: every lock-holding `static` in `crates/lisp/src` (50)
is listed with its reason — pid-keyed, one per machine, a fact of the binary, a pure cache, a
diagnostic, a speed heuristic — and an unlisted one fails the build.

**Tests that waited on time.** A read of all 121 `(sleep N)` in `tests/` found 55 that stood
in for "wait until the other side has done X" — 28 of them able to fail outright under load,
four able to hang (an unbounded `receive` behind the sleep). Each is a handshake now: a
message, a monitor's `[:down]`, a synchronous call as a barrier, or the two new `test`
helpers, `eventually` and `await-parked`. Every rewritten HIGH site was checked by delaying
the other side 1.5 s: the new version passes, and the original failed where that was cheap
to show. `debug_test`'s own poll gave up after a fixed second (200 × 5 ms) and ten of its
`receive`s had no deadline; the poll is `eventually` now and every `receive` has one — with
the first park delayed 1.5 s the old file fails and the new one passes. The runner also says
what a timed-out test was stuck on — the worker and every live
process it spawned, as *running*, *waiting with N unmatched messages* or *waiting with
nothing queued* — read before the kill.

**A soak for the two never-root-caused hangs.** `make runtime-soak` / the nightly's new
30-minute job boots `stress/runtime_soak.blsp` fresh round after round: 240 processes racing
first module loads (KI-127's shape), warm spawn bursts (KI-88's), link churn, large copies,
cycling tiers, one worker and the GC verifier, failing on a lost process, a hang or any
watchdog line, and keeping a record of every round. A loom model of the park handshake was
considered and not built: every park is timed (10 ms with work queued), so a lost wakeup there
costs milliseconds, not KI-88's seconds.

**Running out of descriptors.** Probing under `ulimit -n`: an outbound connect, `file/spit`,
`os/cmd` and `os/run-process` all raise a catchable error naming `EMFILE` and recover. A
listener did not (KI-227): its edge-triggered drain stopped on `EMFILE` and the backlog waited
for a new connection to re-arm it, forever if none came; it is retried every 100 ms now and
logs once per episode. And removing the sleep from `tcp_test`'s closing peer exposed KI-228:
a close with unread input sent a RESET, so a client that read after a pause lost 72 KB of a
200 KB response. The close now shuts the write half and drains until the peer's EOF (bounded
by `LINGER`); a listener starved for descriptors releases lingering sockets at once.

**Left as a decision.** An unbounded program with no `BROOD_MEM_LIMIT` under a real memory cap
still ends in a bare Rust allocation abort (or the OS's kill) with nothing naming the process
that grew; the test runners set a default, `brood`/`nest run` do not.

## 2026-10-13 — the preemption work, simplified: one native entry, one quantum module, folds walked in spans

The brief after the preemption work (`67a540e1`): "less complexity, more cohesion and less
coupling." That work had spread two concerns across too many places, and this pass gives each
one home without changing behaviour.

- **One native entry (ADR-401).** Five Rust paths entered native code — the driver's tier-in,
  the slow call out of native code, the fast link, a builtin's callback (`hof_apply_native`),
  and the inline path's cold funnel — and each carried its own ceremony (env, name, IC cursors,
  depth, gateway, context) and its own outcome handling (unwind record, error, tail chain,
  checkpoint resume, VM re-run). They now share `jit_runtime/entry.rs`: `enter_native`,
  `native_feedback`, `finish_nested`. Two of the old copies ran their re-entries with the
  native depth already lowered, the KI-11 shape; the shared finish raises it for all. The
  unwind bit is passed as an argument from the calling context
  (`JitCallCtx::children_may_unwind`), so `Heap::jit_entry_unwind` and `Heap::jit_child_unwind`
  are gone. Net −460 lines in the five paths, +250 for the module.
- **One quantum module (ADR-403).** The budget, the owed preemption and every preemption
  decision — the driver's safepoint, the eval loop's tick, a native activation's poll, a
  fold's stop — moved into `process/scheduler/quantum.rs`; the driver's two safepoints and the
  native poll callback now only ask it. The driver follows the rule native code already did: a
  spent budget yields only while a process is waiting for a worker.
- **Folds walked in spans (ADR-403).** The resumable folds left where they stopped in a
  thread-local that the prelude read back through `%fold-resume-at` — Rust and Brood coupled
  through hidden state. `%vector-fold-span` / `%range-fold-span` return `[acc next]` instead,
  and `fold`'s walk is a plain Brood loop. `%fold-resume-at`, `resumable_should_stop` and the
  `REQUESTED` flag are gone; the owed bit has three states, which is what "stop once per debt"
  needs.
- Two tests assumed a lone process gets preempted: `preemption.rs`' endless hog (now finite,
  and the responder must reply BEFORE it finishes — a finite hog alone would have passed with
  preemption broken) and `quantum_tail_panic.rs` (four spinners on `BROOD_J=1`).
- The `reverse` vector test used `(%eq (type-of coll) :vector)`, which the checker cannot
  narrow on; `vector?` can.
- **`:long-schedule` across a restart (ADR-404).** A quantum that runs on because nothing is
  waiting restarts the timer; the first cut threw the segment away, so one long `sort` in a
  lone process went unreported (`sysmon_test` caught it). The restart now reports the segment
  it ends if that ran long — a budget's worth of work taking past the threshold is code that
  could not be preempted — so the latency probe counts ~10–25 long quanta a run where it
  counted ~5–15.

**Measured** (instructions, against `67a540e1`, the run pre-flight off): fib, bintree, sort,
collatz level; json −4%; `reduce` +4% and `nqueens` +5% — a span result allocated per fold.
Two first attempts were worse and were measured out: a Brood loop calling a closure reducer per
element (+24% `nqueens` — a computed-head call from native code has no inline cache, so it
resolves the arm every call where the native loop resolves it once), and a counted loop with a
separate element counter (+26% `reduce`).

**Gates:** `make test-both` 1829/1829 on both engines, `make gcstress` clean, fuzz 13
generators × 100 (1 900 programs) 0 divergences / 0 crashes / stale=0, clippy `--all-features`
clean, `make latency-gate` ok (worst p99 84 µs; ten interleaved runs against `67a540e1`: p99 and
p99.9 within run-to-run noise).

## 2026-10-13 (later) — a sweep of the gates that are not `make test`

Everything `make test` does not cover, run on purpose after the simplification:

- **`make ab`'s stdlib-image guard had been off for a week.** Its probe printed through
  `%print`, removed in the whole-project cleanup (`25d72016`); the probe errored on every binary,
  both arms read `unknown`, and the "image on BOTH arms" guard warned symmetrically instead of
  stopping. Worse, the harness never wrote an image for a baseline worktree, so every baseline
  booted from source — ~70 ms a run, which read as a 40-50% win for the working tree on every row
  that afternoon. The probe uses `io/puts`, and the harness writes each arm's image first.
- **`make check-stress` failed on a lean build** — `dev/gc-stats` reaches `%gc-stats`, which only
  `dev-tools` registers, and the gate knew only absent MODULES. A primitive the tree registers
  only under `dev-tools` is now a named skip; any other unbound name still fails (all three cases
  checked). CI runs the gate on the full build, so this was local-only.
- **Warnings in three feature builds**: `--no-default-features` (the preempt-unwind signal, the
  mailbox-overflow peek and the native poll's re-exports, all `jit`-only), `gui` without
  `gui-gpu` (the GPU target's two re-exports), and wasm32 (`OwnedTable` methods only the TCP/TLS
  host uses). `%jit-arm-state` no longer mutates its counters under one feature.
- Also run, all green: `check-examples`, `tier-audit` (29 rows, every hot arm native),
  `check-imaged`, `breakagetests` (37/37), the distribution tests five times (59/59 each), and the
  fold/preemption paths under `BROOD_GC_STRESS=1 BROOD_GC_VERIFY=1` at every tier with a
  100-reduction quantum.
- **Not brood:** bedit at CI's pin passes 2463/2464 with a grammar-enabled `nest`; the one failure
  is its Elixir agent leaving the `dbg` header in place on this machine's Elixir 1.21-dev, which
  wraps the header in ANSI colour. `make wasm-test` needs wasm-bindgen-cli 0.2.128 (0.2.100 is
  installed here); the wasm32 build itself is clean.

## 2026-10-13 (later) — the fold span result stops allocating; a comment moves instruction counts 2%

Handoff item B. `%vector-fold-span` / `%range-fold-span` returned `[acc next]`, a vector
allocated on every fold although almost every fold is one span. A span that finishes now
returns the accumulator itself; one that stops short (4 096 elements, or a preemption owed
between two elements) returns `(:%fold-more acc . next)`, and `fold` tests `pair?` then the
tag. The walk helpers take that continuation directly. `reverse`'s vector branch called
`%vector-fold` with the old signature and now goes through `fold`. ADR-403 updated.

- **The tag.** The first cut tagged with the span native itself, read in Rust by a global
  lookup: `env_get` per stopped span, and `%eq` on two natives runs the structural `equal`
  (~95 instructions). A keyword is an immediate compare. Neither is unforgeable — a private
  prelude def is reachable by name (`%fold-span` reads 4096 from user code) — so the bar is
  "no accidental collision", which an internal keyword meets.
- **Numbers** (`instructions:u`, stdimage live on both arms): `nqueens` 1 204.1M → 1 188.9M
  (−1.3%); a micro of 2M one-span folds −2.7% (~145 instructions a fold); `reduce`, and a
  200M-element range, flat. The stopped-short path costs ~150 instructions more per span than
  the vector did (two pairs, and three `first`/`rest` that are runtime callbacks in this arm —
  it calls a native, so no pair bases are hoisted), once per 4 096 elements.
- **Smaller than the handoff's 5%.** That figure was against the resumable version, which had
  no Brood walk at all; the vector was only part of it. `Reducer::resolve` per span
  (`reduce_prim_op`: `select_arm`, the passthrough map clone, `env_get`, the native-name match)
  is the next per-fold cost, but it did not register in a sampled `nqueens` profile, where the
  JIT arms, `hof_apply_step` and `fill_captures` dominate — item A's territory. Left alone.
- **Measurement lesson.** A control build — HEAD plus one comment line in `core.blsp` — moved
  `nqueens`-class rows by up to 2.3% and boot by 0.7M instructions against HEAD, with no
  semantic change; the same full-source boot agreed to 0.06%. Instruction counts are not
  layout-proof across builds (hashing, allocation addresses, the image's own hash). A delta of
  a few percent between two builds needs a control build of its own before it means anything;
  per-operation costs measured inside one build (an amplified row such as `%fold-span` rebound
  to 1) are the reliable figures.
- **Tests:** `tests/sequence_test.blsp` gains a multi-span fold (vector, range, negative step,
  an accumulator shaped like the continuation but untagged) and a concurrent one (eight workers
  folding 30 000 elements with a closure, so spans stop mid-way under preemption). Sabotage:
  dropping the continuation reds both; a mid-span stop that skips one element reds the
  concurrent test 3/3. A fold program at `BROOD_TIER=0|1|2`, with and without
  `BROOD_GC_STRESS=1 BROOD_GC_VERIFY=1`, `BROOD_REDUCTIONS=100` and `%fold-span` rebound to 3
  (ranges to `i64::MAX`, negative steps, BigInt promotion): identical output on all six and
  equal to HEAD's. `preemption_shapes.rs`/`preemption.rs` 10/10.

## 2026-10-14 — the soft memory limit stops the process holding the memory (KI-229)

Looking at whether `brood`/`nest run` should default a memory ceiling, the first question was
what a ceiling does to a program with more than one process. Under `BROOD_MEM_LIMIT=1G`, a
process keeping a 1 MB string every few milliseconds beside a busy worker that allocated only
garbage: the busy worker died with `E0043`, three runs in three, and the leak kept going. The
soft limit counts the whole allocator and raised in whichever process reached a safepoint
first, and a busy process reaches one far more often than a process that accumulates in short
bursts between receives. A default ceiling on top of that would make every leak kill its
bystanders first.

ADR-399: the crossing stops the largest holder. `Heap::footprint` measures what a heap holds
— both generations' slab capacity plus what entries own: spilled vectors, map and frame
arrays, string text, shared blobs, ropes (the existing `local_bytes` counts only slab slots,
and misses a 1 MB string entirely). Only a process may walk its own heap while it runs, so in
the last eighth of the limit each process measures itself at most every 50 ms, at its quantum
safepoint or when it parks; at the crossing the selector measures parked processes under
their mailbox lock, adds each mailbox's queued wire messages, and stops the largest with a
hard exit whose reason is the `E0043` error map, plus one stderr line naming it. On the leak
probe it measured 738 MiB for a process holding about 740 MB.

Two first attempts failed in instructive ways. Collecting fresh figures in a 20 ms window
after the crossing let a mailbox flood cover the remaining quarter to the hard limit inside
the window — an allocation abort. And without a measurement at park, the leaking process
(which runs for microseconds between `receive`s and never reaches the once-per-quantum
safepoint) published nothing, so the selector found no holder worth stopping and fell back to
raising in the busy worker — one run in three.

Guard: `crates/cli/tests/memory_pressure.rs` — a leak beside a busy worker and a flooded sink
beside a ticker, each asserting the culprit's `[:down …]` carries `E0043` and the bystander
is alive; ten runs green, and both red with the old raise-in-place restored.

The default-ceiling question is still open, but it is now a question about the number, not
about whether a ceiling would do harm.

## 2026-10-14 — native code memoises calls to a closure value (ADR-406); KI-239 filed

Handoff item A. A computed-head call from native code — `(f acc x)`, every callback a
higher-order function calls — re-derived the callee's arm, env and IC block on every call.
`jit_dispatch_call` now memoises them per closure instance and argc (eight direct-mapped
slots), stamped with the code epoch and the collection count; the table lives inside the
body cache's cell, whose mutable accessor drops it, so no write to the cache can leave a
stale memo. ADR-406.

- **Numbers** (`instructions:u` per call, steady state from a 1M/5M-iteration difference,
  both binaries with their stdlib image): anonymous closure 943 → 706, capturing 1 058 →
  817, a global function passed as a value 1 057 → 706; a named head unchanged at 415. Rows
  against a HEAD control: `persistent-map` −3.3%, `base64` −2.4%, `primes` −1.7%, `ring`
  −1.8%, `nqueens` −1.2%, the rest within ±1%; `spawn` read +4.8% once and is noise (six
  runs each: 1 225–1 390M on both binaries).
- **What a profile of the first numbers hid.** The obvious micro — `(apply-loop (fn (a x) (+ a
  x)) …)` — cost ~1 900 instructions a call, named or not, and the profile was all `dispatch`,
  `env_get` and `prim_add`: that body is a thin wrapper (ADR-069), which the dispatcher never
  runs — it redirects to `+` by name on every call. The call cache never sees it. A
  non-wrapper body is what measures item A. The wrapper cost is real and common
  (`(fn (acc x) (+ acc x))` is the canonical reducer) and goes to the handoff.
- **Two measurement traps hit again.** A first per-call figure of 296 was taken against a stale
  stdlib image (std loaded from source); and the first run after a rebuild pays the boot-cache
  rebuild, which skews a two-size difference. A HEAD worktree under `target/ab/` with its own
  image was the control that settled both.
- **The memory ratchet caught the first cut.** The table inline in every heap was ~600 B per
  process (`process_floor`: 4 510 against a 4 000 bound). Boxed and allocated at first use,
  then folded into the body-cache cell (no separate generation counter, no `RefCell` flag): 16 B
  per process. HEAD measured 3 976–3 985, which left 4 B of a 4 000 bound against ~9 B of
  jitter, so the bound is 4 020 with the sentence the file asks for.
- **Tests:** `tests/jit_computed_call_test.blsp` — instances of one template alternating
  through one call site, two templates through one site, one closure at two arities, a closure
  made fresh every iteration (collected and moved), and eight processes doing the same at once;
  each loop arm is settled native first. Sabotage: dropping the instance key reds four of five
  (the arity case is guarded by the argc key: dropping THAT crashes the process — the wrong arm
  with the wrong frame). Dropping the epoch and body-cache stamps together reds nothing and
  changes no instruction count, even with a module loaded mid-loop (ADR-366): native entry
  re-checks the arm's own `compile_epoch`, so a stale memo would be slow, not wrong — the
  stamps are recorded as an argument from construction, not as tested. At `BROOD_TIER=0|1|2`
  and under `BROOD_GC_STRESS=1 BROOD_GC_VERIFY=1`: 5/5. `make test-both`, `make gcstress`, fuzz
  (`tier_transition`, `metamorphic`, `pipeline`: 1 350 programs, clean), `make tier-audit` (29
  rows native), `frame_size_callsites` and the JIT/preemption/differential Rust tests.
- **KI-239 (watching).** `test-both`'s VM half had `a_dropped_send_to_an_unregistered_name_warns_once`
  fail try 1: A's `node/connect` was refused right after `wait_until_listening` had reached B.
  KI-99's test, a new mechanism. 40/40 on demand (20 with all cores busy); the test's failure
  message now says whether B was alive and still listening once A finished.

ADR-400..406**. The commit messages written before it (`67a540e1` "ADR-399..404, KI-223..231",
`95c8b749`, `39710846`, `1869e297` "ADR-405; KI-232") carry the OLD numbers — read them through
this map. Conflicts were mechanical: both sides appended to the docs; origin made `is_dynamic`

## 2026-10-14 (later) — deep data, refused threads, fast allocators, and a default memory ceiling

Four probes, four fixes (KI-240..243), and the default ceiling (ADR-407). Written on a branch
from `d7625f28` as KI-230..233 and ADR-400 and renumbered past the preemption merge before
landing; the code merged with `scheduler/quantum.rs`, where `expire_quantum` now lives.

**Deep data.** Every Rust walker a value reaches — printing, equality, hashing, sort, JSON,
send in both directions, `def`, table put and key, the collector, `postwalk`, `flatten` —
ran over a list, vector, map and pair chain nested a million deep, and sets 30 000 deep.
None crashed: each either finished or raised a catchable error (`json/encode` and
`seq/postwalk` hit the recursion limits). The finding was elsewhere: a dotted pair could not
be sent or stored at all, "improper list" from both copy paths, though the language documents
it as a value (KI-240). `Message::Dotted` carries the tail as the last item, so every walker
treats it as a flat container; the node wire gained a tag (protocol v10). Building a deeply
nested *set* is quadratic — each level hashes its whole element, 15 s at depth 20 000 — which
is a cost, not a crash, and is left as it is.

**Refused threads.** A user namespace (`unshare -U`) gives a program its own thread count, so
`ulimit -u N` there is an exact budget on any machine. Under it, `os/spawn` panicked after its
child had started, `offload` and the first `tcp/listen` panicked, startup panicked with a
backtrace, and with no scheduler worker the runtime hung without a word (KI-241). A child's
threads are now reserved before the child exists (`IoThread`); the offload pool and the
reactor start lazily and return the refusal as an error; the lazily-started singletons (JIT
compiler, heartbeat) no longer panic inside a `LazyLock`/`Once`, which would have poisoned
them for good.

**Fast allocators.** Building a test for a victim stuck in a native call turned up a larger
gap: the stuck victim's loop, allocating a megabyte an iteration, ran from the soft limit to
the hard abort by itself, because the VM checks the soft limit once per quantum and a native
loop once per 128 iterations (KI-242). The allocator now ends the thread's quantum on a large
allocation over the limit — `REDUCTIONS` is a `const` thread-local, safe to touch there — and
within the last eighth once per 1/64 of the limit, so a fast hoarder also measures itself for
the selector; a native arm hears of it when its builtin call returns. The first version only
fired over the limit, and the hoarder crossed the near band in fourteen iterations without
publishing a figure; the test hoarded to 350 MB under a 384 MiB limit and never entered the
band at all (352 MB is seven eighths of 384 MiB). The hard abort, when it still comes, now
says which limit and why, from a stack buffer — it runs inside the allocator. Then the stuck
victim itself (KI-243): the selector stopped nobody while its victim lived, and a victim in a
native call lives until the call returns; after 250 ms it is now skipped. The leak test of
KI-229 had to grow in pieces under 64 KiB: with the hook, a leak growing by megabytes noticed
the crossing itself and raised in place, which is right but no longer tested that a
bystander who notices does not pay.

**The default ceiling.** With no `BROOD_MEM_LIMIT`, a process in a memory-capped cgroup takes
its hard limit from the tightest `memory.max` on its path. Seven eighths of the cap was the
first choice and was wrong: under a 600 MB `systemd-run` scope the leak was out-of-memory
killed before the soft limit tripped, because the container counts resident memory and the
allocator counts live bytes (resident ran at about 1.45× live). At three quarters, caps of
600 MB, 1 GB and 2 GB each ended the leak with `E0043`; with the limits off it was killed,
exit 137, nothing printed. This machine's user slice carries a 20 GiB cap, so a plain `brood`
here now has a 15 GiB ceiling; the test runners keep their 3 GiB.

Also: `nest explain`'s entry for blocking natives advertised `(proc/offload …)`, which is
unbound; the function is the bare `offload`.

**Then, after the merge** — light probes only, the machine being in use. Descriptors: under
`ulimit -n 64`, `os/spawn`, `spawn-pty`, `os/cmd`, file reads and listens each fail with a
catchable `Too many open files`, and 200 children, 200 failed spawns and 200 `os/cmd`s in
sequence leak nothing. A full disk (`/dev/full`): file writes raise `No space left on
device`, the images are written to a temporary file and renamed, but stdout dropped its
errors and the run exited 0 (KI-244). Panics from Brood: the builtins' `expect`s are all
behind the type checks they rely on; the tree-sitter and GUI key-name tables took their
locks with `.lock().expect`, so one panic while holding one would have made every later
parse panic too — they use the poison-tolerant `core::sync::lock` like everything else.
Deep data in Brood: `json/decode` handles 100 000 levels; `json/encode` and `seq/postwalk`
raise a catchable `recursion too deep` at a million, and stay as they are — rewriting a
benchmarked encoder is not something to do without measuring it. The non-Unix `os/cmd`
starts its pipe threads with `Builder::spawn` and reaps the child on a refusal; checking it
through a `wasm32` build (also non-Unix) caught `1 << 62` in the new cgroup reader, which
does not fit a 32-bit `usize` — it reads the cap as `u64` now. A fuzz-differential pass on
the merged tree (`metamorphic`, `pipeline`, `tier_transition`, `trycatch`, `match`,
`strings`; 300 programs, four engine configs each): clean.

The pre-push gate on the merged tree then failed `doc_examples_test` on address space —
1 324 threads, all belonging to 317 zombie `sh` children. A logging `sh` on `PATH` named the
`os/spawn` example, run 484 times: a `receive` under `reflect/eval` suspended to the body
driver, which captured at the call into the native and re-ran the whole form on resume
(KI-245). `compile::run` was the one native entry to evaluation that left the capture flag
set; it takes the tree-walker's guard now. The plain double run predates the merge; the
merge's folds-in-the-driver extended it to a `map` callback, which is what the doctest hit.

## 2026-10-14 (later) — thin-wrapper callbacks from native code (ADR-408); KI-239 fixed; the format check ignores what git ignores

Four follow-ups to the merge.

- **Handoff item C (ADR-408).** A thin wrapper — `(fn (acc x) (+ acc x))`, the canonical
  reducer — never ran its own body: the dispatcher redirected it to `+` by name, and `+`'s
  two-argument arm is itself a wrapper of `%add`, so a call from native code took two by-name
  hops (~1 900 instructions) and the wrapper's arm never got hot enough to tier. Native code
  now runs the wrapper's compiled arm like any closure's (`jit_dispatch_call`'s slow path); it
  tiers, and later calls take the native link. Per call from a native loop: anonymous wrapper
  1 892 → 714, a global wrapper passed as a value 2 120 → 714. Rows, A/B of one tree with the
  path on and off: flat but `latency` (~−4% instructions, 5 runs each); `json` is bimodal
  (~1 588M or ~1 670M a run) on BOTH binaries — 11/5 off, 10/6 on over 16 runs, so not this.
  The contract boundary (ADR-383) holds because the compiled body applies it at compile time;
  `contract_test` gained the hot-loop case (a named wrapper, and a wrapper callback, inside
  the contracted module) — red under `BROOD_NO_CONTRACT_BOUNDARY=1`. That case first failed
  on a FIXED total: the neighbouring reload test swapped `f`'s body mid-loop, which is late
  binding working; it now asserts only that the boundary holds. `jit_computed_call_test`: the
  wrapper reaches `:native`, and a `def` of its inner function is seen through it — both red
  with the path disabled (the wrapper stays `:untried`).
- **KI-239 fixed — the test, not the runtime.** B lived for a fixed `(sleep 6000)`; under a
  loaded `test-both` A's boot outlasted it and dialled a node that had exited. Reproduced on
  demand by delaying A 7 s (old test: `Connection refused`, `left: 0`; fixed test: green). B
  now serves until killed and A waits for a pong — a link delivers in order, so the pong
  proves the drop warning was printed.
- **`nest format` skips git-ignored files.** The pre-push hook's whole-project check failed on
  `stress/fuzz_out/fuzz_6.blsp`, a fuzz output no commit can contain. `format-every-blsp` asks
  `git check-ignore --stdin` once and drops what it names (a tracked file never is; outside a
  repo nothing is dropped). In Brood, over `os/cmd`, not a new primitive. Guard: a throwaway
  git project with an unformatted file under an ignored directory passes the check and fails
  it without the ignore rule — red with the filter disabled.
- **`thread_exhaustion`'s no-worker case passed only without a stdlib image** (found by the
  post-merge `test-both`, red on both engines). It ran `brood` under a three-thread budget and
  expected no scheduler worker to start; whether one did depended on whether the JIT compiler
  thread had taken the third slot first, which it did only when the boot compiled something —
  loading std from SOURCE. With a live image a worker started and the runtime ran: tier 0 failed
  always (no JIT thread), tier 2 failed once the image was live, and a fresh worktree (no image)
  passed, which is how it shipped green. Two threads is the budget that means "no worker" on
  every tier and boot path (probed: all six combinations). Still a guard: with the runtime's
  `live == 0` exit disabled it hangs to the 60 s kill and fails.
- **`interp_teardown` gave the reap two seconds.** It failed once in a `test-both` tree-walker
  run at 2.655 s — its 2 s polling deadline plus setup — and passed 50/50 on demand (20 under
  full load). The reap of a dropped runtime's parked processes is asynchronous; the deadline is
  now 30 s, which a pass never waits for. `process_floor` also went red at `ae4e69a3`: the
  other line's KI-246 hash memo, fixed by its own `d6c3db0a` (8 bytes a process, not 72) — which
  left the figure at 4 020–4 021 against a 4 020 bound, two 8 B memo pointers on top of 3 996. The
  bound is 4 040, with the sentence the file asks for.
- **The renumbering commit stripped zero-padding** (`a279e76c`): its regex re-printed every
  matched number as an integer, so `ADR-073` became `ADR-73` in the 53 files it touched — 1 254
  lines, already pushed. The check that commit ran normalised numbers before comparing, so it
  could not see it. Repaired by re-deriving the correct renumbering of the pre-renumber text and
  applying only the differing lines; verified exactly for the 31 files the merge did not also
  touch, and line by line against both sides for the other 22 (one half-repaired line fixed by
  hand).

## 2026-10-14 (evening) — nested-set hashing, tables under the memory limit, a resized container

**Quadratic hashing (KI-246).** A set or map's structural hash walked everything below it
every time, so nesting `n` levels cost O(n²): 15 s at depth 20 000. Keeping a running hash
per CHAMP node was the other design — it would have touched every trie operation and, for
maps, hashed every value on every `assoc`. Instead `HashMemo` remembers the hash of a LOCAL
set or map that took 64+ steps, stamped with `gc_runs`; small keys pay a counter increment.
Measured by the hash's own step count, not a clock: 3 000 levels cost ~100 000 steps (each
level re-walks at most down to the nearest remembered one), against 4 504 500 without it.

**Tables.** A table belongs to no process (ADR-107), so the soft limit can stop nobody to
free what one holds, and its `E0043` named nothing. It now measures the tables
(`table::largest_table`, the stored entries' message sizes) and names the largest in the
hint. Filling a table with megabyte strings first raised through the "the current process
is the largest" branch, not the unattributable one: the filler's own heap still held its
copies of the shared blobs until the next collection. Both branches name the table now.
Owner-death collection of tables stays a language question.

**A resized container.** The cap was read once at startup; a pod resized in place kept its
old limits, and a lowered cap left them above it. It is re-read at most every 5 s. The first
hook, the VM's once-per-quantum check, never ran in the probe — its loop ran native and
otherwise waited in `receive` — so the native fresh-quantum point and the park re-read it too.
Cutting a `systemd-run` scope from 2 GB to 600 MB while the program ran moved its hard limit
from 1 536 to 450 MiB within seconds.

Also: ADR-406's closing sentence about thin wrappers, re-worded in the session that added
ADR-408, had landed as a loose paragraph after ADR-407; it is back in ADR-406.

CI then failed `process_floor`: a parked process cost 4 078 B against its 4 020 B floor —
the memo's `RefCell<HashMemo>` and work counter sat inline in every heap (~72 B), and every
spawn pays for the `Heap` struct. The memo is now a `OnceCell<Box<…>>`, allocated the first
time a hash is worth remembering (8 B inline), and the work counter is a thread-local. The
floor test had not been in the targeted set run before the push; it belongs there for any
change to `Heap`'s fields.
